On zero-shot DL3DV, it leads PSNR within this group at every scale and ranks first or second on SSIM/LPIPS. A coverage-hole analysis shows fewer uncovered pixels than both baselines under high-resolution re-rendering.</p></details> | Accepted to ACCV 2026. 16 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables. Project page: https://janghunn.github.io/pavesplat/ ; code: https://github.com/janghunn/pavesplat |
| **[A Brief Overview of Non-Terrestrial Networks (Updated 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25984v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This article extends the 2023 preprint \emph{A brief introduction to satellite communications for Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN)} (\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.04590v1}{arXiv:2305.04590v1}) with a revision of its foundations, tables and state of the art as of 18 November 2026. It revisits propagation, link budgets, spectrum reuse and phased arrays, including corrected equations and numerical examples. Updated tables distinguish operational, launched and planned constellations and compare published fixed-terminal rates with direct-to-device services. The discussion incorporates developments in 3GPP Releases 17--20, commercial LTE-based direct-to-cell services and research towards 6G non-terrestrial networks. Operator announcements are distinguished from independent measurements and future plans. Open challenges include spectrum coordination, Doppler and timing compensation, mobility, capacity, security and the sustainability of low Earth orbit.</p></details> |  |
| **[GeoGSplat: Geometry-Grounded Non-Pixel-Aligned Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36760v2)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting enables efficient novel view synthesis, but pixel-aligned prediction makes the primitive count grow with image resolution and view count, leading to substantial redundancy and increased computational cost. Non-pixel-aligned methods address this limitation by predicting a fixed number of Gaussians. Without explicit ray and depth constraints, these methods yield misplaced Gaussian centers, causing rendering artifacts under large viewpoint changes. To address this issue, we introduce GeoGSplat, a geometry-grounded feed-forward model that explicitly incorporates geometric priors into the non-pixel-aligned framework. A dual-branch encoder constructs Anchors and Triplane to capture coarse 3D structure and fine-grained scene features, respectively. Subsequently, a hierarchical decoder progressively decodes dense 3D points and Gaussian primitives, enabling 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. GeoGSplat surpasses both state-of-the-art pixel-aligned and non-pixel-aligned Gaussian Splatting methods on DTU and NRGBD in terms of novel view synthesis and reconstruction. The advantage of our geometry-grounded paradigm further increases as target viewpoints move farther from the input cameras, demonstrating stronger generalization to large viewpoint changes. Project Page: https://comedy0913.github.io/projects/geogsplat</p></details> |  |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[OctreeSplat: Hierarchical Octree-Guided Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02658v3)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has shown considerable potential for novel view synthesis (NVS) and 3D reconstruction. Feed-forward methods avoid costly per-scene optimization, but fixed image or spatial resolutions limit their ability to allocate primitives according to local complexity. We present OctreeSplat, a hierarchical octree-guided feed-forward Gaussian splatting. OctreeSplat extracts multi-view features and geometric priors from a frozen visual foundation model (VFM), constructs a hierarchical octree, and predicts densification scores. The resulting representation allocates Gaussian primitives at spatially varying densities according to local complexity. Stochastic solids volume rendering improves depth rendering, strengthens geometric supervision, and supports surface reconstruction. When camera parameters are available, an optional test-time optimization (TTO) module improves agreement between predicted and ground-truth parameters. Training updates only 76 million additional parameters and requires 35 GPU-hours. Experiments on Replica, TUM, Tanks and Temples, and DTU demonstrate competitive RGB view synthesis, depth synthesis, and surface reconstruction. OctreeSplat reduces the number of Gaussian primitives while maintaining high reconstruction quality.</p></details> |  |

## pose-free 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Patient-Zero: Scaling Synthetic Patient Agents to Real-World Distributions without Real Patient Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11078v3)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Synthetic data generation with Large Language Models (LLMs) has emerged as a promising solution in the medical domain to mitigate data scarcity and privacy constraints. However, existing approaches remain constrained by their derivative nature, relying on real-world records, which pose privacy risks and distribution biases. Furthermore, current patient agents face the Stability-Plasticity Dilemma, struggling to maintain clinical consistency during dynamic inquiries. To address these challenges, we introduce Patient-Zero, a novel framework for ab initio patient simulation that requires no real medical records. Our Medically-Aligned Hierarchical Synthesis framework generates comprehensive and diverse patient records from medical knowledge via stratified attribute permutation. To support rigorous clinical interaction, we design a Dual-Track Cognitive Memory System to enable agents dynamically update memory while preserving logical consistency and persona adherence. Extensive evaluations show that Patient-Zero outperforms baselines in both data quality and interaction fidelity. In physician evaluations, our records obtain the highest mean diversity, consistency, and completeness ratings. Downstream medical reasoning model trained on our synthetic dataset shows substantial performance gains (MedQA +24.0%; MMLU +14.5%), demonstrating the practical utility of our framework.</p></details> | 27 pages, 5 figures. Published in EMNLP 2026 |
| **[Information-Theoretic Generalization for Adaptive Learning Under Privacy and Fairness Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30867v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Adaptive learners may update, select, and stop models after repeatedly examining the same data. Privacy and fairness constraints regulate this adaptation only selectively: they limit information about a private or sensitive coordinate, whereas task generalization depends on information about the coordinate on which loss is evaluated. We develop a unified information-theoretic framework for this mismatch. For a finite-alphabet pair $(A,B)$, we define directly the residual-information certificate $ h_ε(P_{AB})=\min\{H(B),H(B&#124; A)+ε\}. $ A chain-rule transfer inequality converts a bound on $I(W;A)$ into an upper bound on $I(W;B)$, which can be inserted into a mutual-information generalization inequality. We first establish a transcript-level composition theorem for sequential learners. Conditional per-update information budgets add along the transcript, and the resulting certificate remains valid for history-dependent checkpoint selection, bounded stopping, and independent heterogeneous data batches. Under selective privacy, the learner observes $(X^n,Y^n)$, protects $X^n$, and incurs loss on $Y^n$. Under fairness, the learner observes $(S^n,X^n,T^n)$, limits dependence on the sensitive block $S^n$, compresses $X^n$, and incurs loss on $T^n$. The bounds expose the residual entropies $H(Y&#124; X)$, $H(T&#124; S)$, and $H(T&#124; X)$ and the cumulative information cost of adaptation. We retain block and individual-sample bounds, task-compression refinements, per-output total-variation privacy, empirical-to-population demographic-parity transfer, and finite-alphabet information-parity transfer. FRL/EFRL/SFRL/ESFRL constructions study the tightness of the task-information upper bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[Computing $ab+cd$ and other expressions with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30415v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce two ``high-level'' algorithms that make it possible to evaluate the sum $(x\_h+x\_\ell) + (y\_h+y\_\ell)$ of two double-word numbers with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic. These algorithms allow one to evaluate expressions of the form $ab+cd$, $a+b+cd$, $a+b+c+d$, and $abc$ with correct rounding, where $a$, $b$, $c$, and $d$ are floating-point numbers.</p></details> |  |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v3)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Sharp Volume Contraction of a Centrally Cut John Ellipsoid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29323v1)** | 2026-11-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$ in positive dimension, let $w(K)$ be the volume of its maximum-volume inscribed ellipsoid. For a closed halfspace $H$ whose boundary passes through the center of that ellipsoid, we prove \[ w(K\cap H)\le R_nw(K),\qquad R_1=\frac12,\qquad R_n=\frac12\left(\frac n{n-1}\right)^{(n-1)/2}\quad(n\ge2), \] with equality attained in every fixed dimension. This refines the optimal dimension-independent bound from the author's earlier work on [Khachiyan's ellipsoid conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28447). We characterize the full equality family, including nonsimplex extremizers, and establish an exact volume-deficit identity that yields dimension-independent stability of the new ellipsoid and its weighted contact geometry in John coordinates. The proof reduces the problem to finite John contacts and constructs an explicit conic certificate by a transformation of contact directions and a common polar rotation. Its determinant is controlled by a nonnegative contact defect. The sharp contraction theorem, equality classification, stability estimates, and supporting results have been formally verified in Lean 4 using mathlib.</p></details> | Lean 4 formalization with mathlib, see [github repository](https://github.com/DrZhouKarl/SharpContractionCutJohnEllipsoid) |
| **[ADEPT: Adaptive Monocular Depth Estimation via Model-In-The-Loop Photometric Tuning with Capture Risk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27423v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Exposure and gain determine the visual evidence available to monocular depth estimation (MDE), particularly when robot motion creates a tradeoff between light collection and motion blur. We present ADEPT, a framework that adapts these settings using learned feedback from a frozen depth model. During training, ground-truth depth identifies the lowest-error reference within a group of physically evaluated camera settings. A compact predictor learns reference-relative disagreement (RRD) between each candidate's depth prediction and the reference prediction using regression and ranking losses. At deployment, it scores observed captures using frozen-model features and camera, motion, and illumination context, without ground truth or reference images. A contextual Gaussian-process controller uses recent scores to select subsequent exposure and gain settings under motion and photometric constraints. We evaluate offline camera selection with relative and metric Depth Anything v2 and demonstrate online control with the affine-invariant variant on a mobile robot. Across nine equally weighted scene-illumination conditions, ADEPT reduces mean scale-and-shift-aligned $\mathrm{Abs}_{\mathrm{rel}}$ by 14.6% relative to built-in auto-exposure and by 10.0% relative to the best-performing evaluated prior controller.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improved Accuracy Scaling with Problem Complexity in LLM Arithmetic Reasoning Using Executable Symbolic Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27203v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>High accuracy on mathematical benchmarks does not establish whether reasoning remains reliable as problem complexity increases. We examine how executable symbolic representations change accuracy scaling in large language models compared with natural-language reasoning. To address this limitation, we introduce the GSM Arithmetic Benchmark (GSM-AB), a controlled benchmark that independently varies operand digit length and operation count to probe numerical scale and compositional complexity. We find that executable representations can improve accuracy and slow its decline as complexity increases. Lean reduces average sensitivity to operand digit length at every tested model size. By contrast, slower degradation with increasing operation count emerges at the larger model sizes. A Python program-of-thought (Python-PoT) control shows a similar operation-axis pattern, suggesting that this finding is not specific to Lean. These findings show that reasoning representation shapes arithmetic accuracy scaling and support executable symbolic representations for more reliable reasoning on complex arithmetic tasks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Variants of Beam Search for DiskANN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26609v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Graph-based data structures such as DiskANN, HNSW, and NSG constitute the state of the art for large-scale approximate nearest-neighbor (ANN) search. Indyk and Xu introduced the notion of $α$-reachable graphs, which include graphs built by DiskANN's slow-preprocessing construction, and proved that greedy search achieves an approximation factor of $\tfrac{α+1}{α-1}$. Independently, Al-Jazzazi et al. proposed adaptive beam search (ABS) with a relaxation parameter $γ&gt; 0$, showing a $\tfrac{2}γ$ approximation on navigable graphs ($α\approx 1$), where greedy search admits no guarantees. In this work, we unify and generalize these results by proving that ABS with parameter $γ$ on $α$-reachable graphs achieves an approximation of $\tfrac{α+1}{α(γ+1)-1}$ for any metric. Moreover, on sorted $α$-reachable graphs (introduced by Gollapudi et al.) under the $\ell_2$ metric, we obtain the tighter bound $\max\bigl(\tfracα{α(γ+1)-1}, \tfrac{α^{2}+1}{α^{2}(γ+1)-1}\bigr)$. Beyond these theoretical results, we design a beam predictor using features inspired by fine-grained analysis of ABS, trained via a constrained objective that minimizes mean distance computations subject to a target recall. On three 100-million-point benchmarks, using graphs built by DiskANN's practical construction, beam predictor lowers mean query latency on SSD-resident indices at matched recall by up to $29.6\%$ relative to classical beam search (CBS). Relative to ABS, it is faster in $11$ of $15$ settings, by up to $6.5\%$, and at most $1.4\%$ slower in the other $4$; at $95\%$ recall, its advantage over ABS grows from $0.8$--$4.2\%$ for $100$ neighbors to $4.6$--$5.6\%$ for $500$ neighbors. In memory, it needs $1.3$--$23.7\%$ fewer distance computations than CBS and $0.0$--$3.9\%$ fewer than ABS.</p></details> | 22 pages, 1 figure, 9 tables |

Published in EMNLP 2026 |
| **[Enhancing Sparse-View Gaussian Splatting with Feed-Forward Priors via Spectral Fusion and Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31428v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Gaussian splatting from sparse training views renders high-quality images near training views, but reconstruction quality degrades at distant regions, which are undersampled by the training set. Feed-forward novel view synthesis (NVS) models use strong learned priors to resample the scene from substantially different viewpoints, but they take more than ten times as long to render and produce high-frequency artifacts on out-of-domain scenes. We propose a plug-and-play method that enhances an existing sparse-view Gaussian model with a pretrained feed-forward NVS model. We observe that the two are complementary in the frequency domain: the prior is more accurate at low frequencies, and the Gaussians at high frequencies. The reliability of the prior relative to the Gaussians is inversely proportional to the image frequency, so the optimal fusion weight is a first-order low-pass filter with one parameter that can be fitted. Our key idea is then to intelligently fuse the reliable part of each of the two methods. To remove the slow feed-forward model at render time, we use these fused images as teachers and distill them back into Gaussians, balanced with supervision from input views. Our method improves the quality of various sparse-view Gaussian methods without sacrificing rendering speed, and our analysis works on different prior models. Project page: https://linwk20.github.io/spectral-fusion-gs/</p></details> | 15 pages. Project page: https://linwk20.github.io/spectral-fusion-gs/ |

On three 100-million-point benchmarks, using graphs built by DiskANN's practical construction, beam predictor lowers mean query latency on SSD-resident indices at matched recall by up to $29.6\%$ relative to classical beam search (CBS). Relative to ABS, it is faster in $11$ of $15$ settings, by up to $6.5\%$, and at most $1.4\%$ slower in the other $4$; at $95\%$ recall, its advantage over ABS grows from $0.8$--$4.2\%$ for $100$ neighbors to $4.6$--$5.6\%$ for $500$ neighbors. In memory, it needs $1.3$--$23.7\%$ fewer distance computations than CBS and $0.0$--$3.9\%$ fewer than ABS.</p></details> | 22 pages, 1 figure, 9 tables |
| **[A Brief Overview of Non-Terrestrial Networks (Updated 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25984v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This article extends the 2023 preprint \emph{A brief introduction to satellite communications for Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN)} (\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.04590v1}{arXiv:2305.04590v1}) with a revision of its foundations, tables and state of the art as of 18 November 2026. It revisits propagation, link budgets, spectrum reuse and phased arrays, including corrected equations and numerical examples. Updated tables distinguish operational, launched and planned constellations and compare published fixed-terminal rates with direct-to-device services. The discussion incorporates developments in 3GPP Releases 17--20, commercial LTE-based direct-to-cell services and research towards 6G non-terrestrial networks. Operator announcements are distinguished from independent measurements and future plans. Open challenges include spectrum coordination, Doppler and timing compensation, mobility, capacity, security and the sustainability of low Earth orbit.</p></details> |  |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |

## large-scale Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Patient-Zero: Scaling Synthetic Patient Agents to Real-World Distributions without Real Patient Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11078v3)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Synthetic data generation with Large Language Models (LLMs) has emerged as a promising solution in the medical domain to mitigate data scarcity and privacy constraints. However, existing approaches remain constrained by their derivative nature, relying on real-world records, which pose privacy risks and distribution biases. Furthermore, current patient agents face the Stability-Plasticity Dilemma, struggling to maintain clinical consistency during dynamic inquiries. To address these challenges, we introduce Patient-Zero, a novel framework for ab initio patient simulation that requires no real medical records. Our Medically-Aligned Hierarchical Synthesis framework generates comprehensive and diverse patient records from medical knowledge via stratified attribute permutation. To support rigorous clinical interaction, we design a Dual-Track Cognitive Memory System to enable agents dynamically update memory while preserving logical consistency and persona adherence. Extensive evaluations show that Patient-Zero outperforms baselines in both data quality and interaction fidelity. In physician evaluations, our records obtain the highest mean diversity, consistency, and completeness ratings. Downstream medical reasoning model trained on our synthetic dataset shows substantial performance gains (MedQA +24.0%; MMLU +14.5%), demonstrating the practical utility of our framework.</p></details> | 27 pages, 5 figures. Published in EMNLP 2026 |
| **[Information-Theoretic Generalization for Adaptive Learning Under Privacy and Fairness Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30867v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Adaptive learners may update, select, and stop models after repeatedly examining the same data. Privacy and fairness constraints regulate this adaptation only selectively: they limit information about a private or sensitive coordinate, whereas task generalization depends on information about the coordinate on which loss is evaluated. We develop a unified information-theoretic framework for this mismatch. For a finite-alphabet pair $(A,B)$, we define directly the residual-information certificate $ h_ε(P_{AB})=\min\{H(B),H(B&#124; A)+ε\}. $ A chain-rule transfer inequality converts a bound on $I(W;A)$ into an upper bound on $I(W;B)$, which can be inserted into a mutual-information generalization inequality. We first establish a transcript-level composition theorem for sequential learners. Conditional per-update information budgets add along the transcript, and the resulting certificate remains valid for history-dependent checkpoint selection, bounded stopping, and independent heterogeneous data batches. Under selective privacy, the learner observes $(X^n,Y^n)$, protects $X^n$, and incurs loss on $Y^n$. Under fairness, the learner observes $(S^n,X^n,T^n)$, limits dependence on the sensitive block $S^n$, compresses $X^n$, and incurs loss on $T^n$. The bounds expose the residual entropies $H(Y&#124; X)$, $H(T&#124; S)$, and $H(T&#124; X)$ and the cumulative information cost of adaptation. We retain block and individual-sample bounds, task-compression refinements, per-output total-variation privacy, empirical-to-population demographic-parity transfer, and finite-alphabet information-parity transfer. FRL/EFRL/SFRL/ESFRL constructions study the tightness of the task-information upper bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[Computing $ab+cd$ and other expressions with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30415v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce two ``high-level'' algorithms that make it possible to evaluate the sum $(x\_h+x\_\ell) + (y\_h+y\_\ell)$ of two double-word numbers with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic. These algorithms allow one to evaluate expressions of the form $ab+cd$, $a+b+cd$, $a+b+c+d$, and $abc$ with correct rounding, where $a$, $b$, $c$, and $d$ are floating-point numbers.</p></details> |  |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v3)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Sharp Volume Contraction of a Centrally Cut John Ellipsoid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29323v1)** | 2026-11-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$ in positive dimension, let $w(K)$ be the volume of its maximum-volume inscribed ellipsoid. For a closed halfspace $H$ whose boundary passes through the center of that ellipsoid, we prove \[ w(K\cap H)\le R_nw(K),\qquad R_1=\frac12,\qquad R_n=\frac12\left(\frac n{n-1}\right)^{(n-1)/2}\quad(n\ge2), \] with equality attained in every fixed dimension. This refines the optimal dimension-independent bound from the author's earlier work on [Khachiyan's ellipsoid conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28447). We characterize the full equality family, including nonsimplex extremizers, and establish an exact volume-deficit identity that yields dimension-independent stability of the new ellipsoid and its weighted contact geometry in John coordinates. The proof reduces the problem to finite John contacts and constructs an explicit conic certificate by a transformation of contact directions and a common polar rotation. Its determinant is controlled by a nonnegative contact defect. The sharp contraction theorem, equality classification, stability estimates, and supporting results have been formally verified in Lean 4 using mathlib.</p></details> | Lean 4 formalization with mathlib, see [github repository](https://github.com/DrZhouKarl/SharpContractionCutJohnEllipsoid) |
| **[ADEPT: Adaptive Monocular Depth Estimation via Model-In-The-Loop Photometric Tuning with Capture Risk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27423v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Exposure and gain determine the visual evidence available to monocular depth estimation (MDE), particularly when robot motion creates a tradeoff between light collection and motion blur. We present ADEPT, a framework that adapts these settings using learned feedback from a frozen depth model. During training, ground-truth depth identifies the lowest-error reference within a group of physically evaluated camera settings. A compact predictor learns reference-relative disagreement (RRD) between each candidate's depth prediction and the reference prediction using regression and ranking losses. At deployment, it scores observed captures using frozen-model features and camera, motion, and illumination context, without ground truth or reference images. A contextual Gaussian-process controller uses recent scores to select subsequent exposure and gain settings under motion and photometric constraints. We evaluate offline camera selection with relative and metric Depth Anything v2 and demonstrate online control with the affine-invariant variant on a mobile robot. Across nine equally weighted scene-illumination conditions, ADEPT reduces mean scale-and-shift-aligned $\mathrm{Abs}_{\mathrm{rel}}$ by 14.6% relative to built-in auto-exposure and by 10.0% relative to the best-performing evaluated prior controller.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improved Accuracy Scaling with Problem Complexity in LLM Arithmetic Reasoning Using Executable Symbolic Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27203v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>High accuracy on mathematical benchmarks does not establish whether reasoning remains reliable as problem complexity increases. We examine how executable symbolic representations change accuracy scaling in large language models compared with natural-language reasoning. To address this limitation, we introduce the GSM Arithmetic Benchmark (GSM-AB), a controlled benchmark that independently varies operand digit length and operation count to probe numerical scale and compositional complexity. We find that executable representations can improve accuracy and slow its decline as complexity increases. Lean reduces average sensitivity to operand digit length at every tested model size. By contrast, slower degradation with increasing operation count emerges at the larger model sizes. A Python program-of-thought (Python-PoT) control shows a similar operation-axis pattern, suggesting that this finding is not specific to Lean. These findings show that reasoning representation shapes arithmetic accuracy scaling and support executable symbolic representations for more reliable reasoning on complex arithmetic tasks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Variants of Beam Search for DiskANN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26609v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Graph-based data structures such as DiskANN, HNSW, and NSG constitute the state of the art for large-scale approximate nearest-neighbor (ANN) search. On three 100-million-point benchmarks, using graphs built by DiskANN's practical construction, beam predictor lowers mean query latency on SSD-resident indices at matched recall by up to $29.6\%$ relative to classical beam search (CBS). Relative to ABS, it is faster in $11$ of $15$ settings, by up to $6.5\%$, and at most $1.4\%$ slower in the other $4$; at $95\%$ recall, its advantage over ABS grows from $0.8$--$4.2\%$ for $100$ neighbors to $4.6$--$5.6\%$ for $500$ neighbors. In memory, it needs $1.3$--$23.7\%$ fewer distance computations than CBS and $0.0$--$3.9\%$ fewer than ABS.</p></details> | 22 pages, 1 figure, 9 tables |

| **[ST-SfM: Spatio-Temporal Structure-from-Motion for Initialising 4D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32231v1)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dynamic Gaussian splatting is usually initialised either with random points or with a structure-from-motion (SfM) cloud of the first frame. Neither says anything about what moves, where and when. We introduce ST-SfM, a sparse spatio-temporal SfM for synchronised, calibrated multi-camera video that splits the scene into two branches. Static structure is detected once per camera on a temporal-median image, gated for temporal stability and matched along epi-polar lines. Motion is detected as space-time interest points (STIPs) in the video volume, described with HOG3D and matched only between candidates that agree both epipolarly and in time. Union-find tracks that refuse inconsistent merges are triangulated with RANSAC over all view pairs, giving static 3D points and dynamic 4D points (x, y, z, t) with sub-frame times. We use ST-SfM derived sparse 4D cloud to initialise 4D Gaussian splatting. In comparison with random initialisation on N3DV scenes we get improvements in PSNR, SSIM and LPIPS.</p></details> | 9 pages, 5 figures |
| **[UniView: Large-Baseline View Synthesis via Video Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04701v3)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Novel view synthesis from casually captured monocular images and videos offers a practical route to immersive content creation without dedicated multi-view capture. However, the limited viewpoint coverage of such inputs leaves scene geometry insufficiently constrained, restricting the ability of reconstruction-based methods, such as neural radiance fields (NeRF) and 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), to render views far from the observed viewpoints. We present UniView, a unified framework for large-baseline novel view synthesis and scene generation from monocular images and videos. Central to our approach are an occlusion-aware point cloud renderer and a dual-branch conditioning mechanism. The renderer uses triple reprojection to identify and filter unreliable projections under large viewpoint changes. The conditioning mechanism integrates the resulting geometric guidance with source-view appearance within the video diffusion model, enabling precise camera control while preserving visual details and synthesizing unobserved content. To extend view synthesis to scene generation, we introduce a two-stage generation strategy that decouples viewpoint changes from temporal evolution. This strategy produces dense, synchronized multi-view videos that provide additional supervision for dynamic Gaussian Splatting, enabling more complete 4D generation from monocular observations. Extensive evaluations across static and dynamic scenes demonstrate the effectiveness of UniView in novel view synthesis and scene generation, combining precise camera control with geometric consistency and visual fidelity.</p></details> | Project Homepage: https://zhouhyocean.github.io/uniworld-view/ Code: https://github.com/PKU-YuanGroup/UniWorld-View |
| **[Patient-Zero: Scaling Synthetic Patient Agents to Real-World Distributions without Real Patient Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11078v3)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Synthetic data generation with Large Language Models (LLMs) has emerged as a promising solution in the medical domain to mitigate data scarcity and privacy constraints. However, existing approaches remain constrained by their derivative nature, relying on real-world records, which pose privacy risks and distribution biases. To address these challenges, we introduce Patient-Zero, a novel framework for ab initio patient simulation that requires no real medical records. Our Medically-Aligned Hierarchical Synthesis framework generates comprehensive and diverse patient records from medical knowledge via stratified attribute permutation. To support rigorous clinical interaction, we design a Dual-Track Cognitive Memory System to enable agents dynamically update memory while preserving logical consistency and persona adherence. Published in EMNLP 2026 |
| **[Information-Theoretic Generalization for Adaptive Learning Under Privacy and Fairness Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30867v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Adaptive learners may update, select, and stop models after repeatedly examining the same data. For a finite-alphabet pair $(A,B)$, we define directly the residual-information certificate $ h_ε(P_{AB})=\min\{H(B),H(B&#124; A)+ε\}. $ A chain-rule transfer inequality converts a bound on $I(W;A)$ into an upper bound on $I(W;B)$, which can be inserted into a mutual-information generalization inequality. We first establish a transcript-level composition theorem for sequential learners. Conditional per-update information budgets add along the transcript, and the resulting certificate remains valid for history-dependent checkpoint selection, bounded stopping, and independent heterogeneous data batches. Under selective privacy, the learner observes $(X^n,Y^n)$, protects $X^n$, and incurs loss on $Y^n$. Under fairness, the learner observes $(S^n,X^n,T^n)$, limits dependence on the sensitive block $S^n$, compresses $X^n$, and incurs loss on $T^n$. The bounds expose the residual entropies $H(Y&#124; X)$, $H(T&#124; S)$, and $H(T&#124; X)$ and the cumulative information cost of adaptation. We retain block and individual-sample bounds, task-compression refinements, per-output total-variation privacy, empirical-to-population demographic-parity transfer, and finite-alphabet information-parity transfer. FRL/EFRL/SFRL/ESFRL constructions study the tightness of the task-information upper bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[Computing $ab+cd$ and other expressions with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30415v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce two ``high-level'' algorithms that make it possible to evaluate the sum $(x\_h+x\_\ell) + (y\_h+y\_\ell)$ of two double-word numbers with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v3)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Sharp Volume Contraction of a Centrally Cut John Ellipsoid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29323v1)** | 2026-11-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$ in positive dimension, let $w(K)$ be the volume of its maximum-volume inscribed ellipsoid. For a closed halfspace $H$ whose boundary passes through the center of that ellipsoid, we prove \[ w(K\cap H)\le R_nw(K),\qquad R_1=\frac12,\qquad R_n=\frac12\left(\frac n{n-1}\right)^{(n-1)/2}\quad(n\ge2), \] with equality attained in every fixed dimension. This refines the optimal dimension-independent bound from the author's earlier work on [Khachiyan's ellipsoid conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28447). We characterize the full equality family, including nonsimplex extremizers, and establish an exact volume-deficit identity that yields dimension-independent stability of the new ellipsoid and its weighted contact geometry in John coordinates. The proof reduces the problem to finite John contacts and constructs an explicit conic certificate by a transformation of contact directions and a common polar rotation. Its determinant is controlled by a nonnegative contact defect. The sharp contraction theorem, equality classification, stability estimates, and supporting results have been formally verified in Lean 4 using mathlib.</p></details> | Lean 4 formalization with mathlib, see [github repository](https://github.com/DrZhouKarl/SharpContractionCutJohnEllipsoid) |
| **[SemGuard: Semantics-Decoupled Watermarking for Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28636v1)** | 2026-11-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Gaussian Splatting provides an efficient representation for high-fidelity rendering of static and dynamic 3D scenes, motivating the copyright protection of Gaussian assets. In CLIP-guided Gaussian watermarking, a universal message decoder pretrained on textual watermark representations is subsequently applied to visual features containing both watermark information and scene-dependent semantics. However, text-only pretraining does not explicitly account for the semantic interference encountered during watermark extraction. To address this challenge, we propose SemGuard, a simple yet effective semantics-decoupled watermarking framework that learns to suppress scene-dependent semantic interference during universal message decoder pretraining. Specifically, SemGuard combines message-related CLIP text features with message-independent visual features extracted from unwatermarked scene images, constructing training representations with controllable semantic interference relevant to the target Gaussian assets. A progressive semantic interference curriculum gradually increases the contribution of visual features, allowing the decoder to first learn reliable message representations and then adapt to increasingly strong scene-dependent semantic interference. After pretraining for a given message length and semantic image pool, the decoder is frozen and reused across multiple Gaussian assets whose visual semantics are covered by the pretraining data. Experiments on static and dynamic Gaussian Splatting assets demonstrate improved rendering fidelity alongside reliable watermark extraction, while evaluations on static assets further show enhanced robustness against image-space distortions.</p></details> | 10 pages, 3 figures. Submitted to IEEE Transactions on Multimedia (under review) |
| **[ADEPT: Adaptive Monocular Depth Estimation via Model-In-The-Loop Photometric Tuning with Capture Risk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27423v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Exposure and gain determine the visual evidence available to monocular depth estimation (MDE), particularly when robot motion creates a tradeoff between light collection and motion blur. A compact predictor learns reference-relative disagreement (RRD) between each candidate's depth prediction and the reference prediction using regression and ranking losses. At deployment, it scores observed captures using frozen-model features and camera, motion, and illumination context, without ground truth or reference images. A contextual Gaussian-process controller uses recent scores to select subsequent exposure and gain settings under motion and photometric constraints. We evaluate offline camera selection with relative and metric Depth Anything v2 and demonstrate online control with the affine-invariant variant on a mobile robot. Across nine equally weighted scene-illumination conditions, ADEPT reduces mean scale-and-shift-aligned $\mathrm{Abs}_{\mathrm{rel}}$ by 14.6% relative to built-in auto-exposure and by 10.0% relative to the best-performing evaluated prior controller.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improved Accuracy Scaling with Problem Complexity in LLM Arithmetic Reasoning Using Executable Symbolic Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27203v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>High accuracy on mathematical benchmarks does not establish whether reasoning remains reliable as problem complexity increases. We examine how executable symbolic representations change accuracy scaling in large language models compared with natural-language reasoning. To address this limitation, we introduce the GSM Arithmetic Benchmark (GSM-AB), a controlled benchmark that independently varies operand digit length and operation count to probe numerical scale and compositional complexity. We find that executable representations can improve accuracy and slow its decline as complexity increases. Lean reduces average sensitivity to operand digit length at every tested model size. By contrast, slower degradation with increasing operation count emerges at the larger model sizes. A Python program-of-thought (Python-PoT) control shows a similar operation-axis pattern, suggesting that this finding is not specific to Lean. These findings show that reasoning representation shapes arithmetic accuracy scaling and support executable symbolic representations for more reliable reasoning on complex arithmetic tasks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Variants of Beam Search for DiskANN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26609v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Graph-based data structures such as DiskANN, HNSW, and NSG constitute the state of the art for large-scale approximate nearest-neighbor (ANN) search. Indyk and Xu introduced the notion of $α$-reachable graphs, which include graphs built by DiskANN's slow-preprocessing construction, and proved that greedy search achieves an approximation factor of $\tfrac{α+1}{α-1}$. Independently, Al-Jazzazi et al. proposed adaptive beam search (ABS) with a relaxation parameter $γ&gt; 0$, showing a $\tfrac{2}γ$ approximation on navigable graphs ($α\approx 1$), where greedy search admits no guarantees. In this work, we unify and generalize these results by proving that ABS with parameter $γ$ on $α$-reachable graphs achieves an approximation of $\tfrac{α+1}{α(γ+1)-1}$ for any metric. Moreover, on sorted $α$-reachable graphs (introduced by Gollapudi et al.) under the $\ell_2$ metric, we obtain the tighter bound $\max\bigl(\tfracα{α(γ+1)-1}, \tfrac{α^{2}+1}{α^{2}(γ+1)-1}\bigr)$. Beyond these theoretical results, we design a beam predictor using features inspired by fine-grained analysis of ABS, trained via a constrained objective that minimizes mean distance computations subject to a target recall. On three 100-million-point benchmarks, using graphs built by DiskANN's practical construction, beam predictor lowers mean query latency on SSD-resident indices at matched recall by up to $29.6\%$ relative to classical beam search (CBS). Relative to ABS, it is faster in $11$ of $15$ settings, by up to $6.5\%$, and at most $1.4\%$ slower in the other $4$; at $95\%$ recall, its advantage over ABS grows from $0.8$--$4.2\%$ for $100$ neighbors to $4.6$--$5.6\%$ for $500$ neighbors. In memory, it needs $1.3$--$23.7\%$ fewer distance computations than CBS and $0.0$--$3.9\%$ fewer than ABS.</p></details> | 22 pages, 1 figure, 9 tables |
| **[A Brief Overview of Non-Terrestrial Networks (Updated 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25984v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This article extends the 2023 preprint \emph{A brief introduction to satellite communications for Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN)} (\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.04590v1}{arXiv:2305.04590v1}) with a revision of its foundations, tables and state of the art as of 18 November 2026. It revisits propagation, link budgets, spectrum reuse and phased arrays, including corrected equations and numerical examples. Updated tables distinguish operational, launched and planned constellations and compare published fixed-terminal rates with direct-to-device services. The discussion incorporates developments in 3GPP Releases 17--20, commercial LTE-based direct-to-cell services and research towards 6G non-terrestrial networks. Operator announcements are distinguished from independent measurements and future plans. Open challenges include spectrum coordination, Doppler and timing compensation, mobility, capacity, security and the sustainability of low Earth orbit.</p></details> |  |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |
| **[CueSplat: Learning to Schedule Sparse Deformation for Dynamic Gaussian Splatting on Edge Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22095v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Deformation-based 4D Gaussian splatting (4DGS) enables dynamic scene rendering. Thus, we propose CueSplat, a post-training framework that exploits local temporal regularity while keeping the pretrained backbone frozen and retaining its Gaussian set. Specifically, CueSplat predicts Gaussian attributes from recent history and uses a lightweight learned router to select exact deformation updates within a scene-specific budget calibrated offline. Selection accounts for prediction reliability and rendering contribution. Selected indices are reused across nearby frames to reduce routing overhead, while selected Gaussians still receive exact updates each frame. To control accumulated prediction error, we use drift signals to schedule three consecutive full-deformation frames that rebuild the prediction history. Evaluated across four mainstream 4DGS algorithms, CueSplat achieves up to $6.74\times$ and $8.15\times$ speedups on Jetson AGX Orin and Orin NX, respectively, with a negligible quality drop (at most $0.16$ dB in dataset-average PSNR).</p></details> | 10 pages, 6 figures. Main paper only |

| **[GOLD-SLAM: Gaussian-splatting Omnidirectional LiDAR-Depth SLAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32335v1)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Panoramic cameras capture nearly the entire spherical field of view in a single frame. However, the mismatch between this panoramic coverage and the field of view of LiDAR poses a substantial challenge for panoramic Gaussian splatting SLAM that relies on LiDAR depth priors. We introduce GOLD-SLAM, a Gaussian-splatting Omnidirectional LiDAR-Depth SLAM system that combines high-quality rendering with accurate geometric reconstruction. It performs tracking and mapping in the spherical domain and uses tracked visual features in regions without LiDAR coverage to improve pose estimation. To address insufficient coverage of newly observed surfaces and floating artifacts during incremental mapping, we introduce Depth-Aware Map Extension and Pruning (DAEP). To enable broader evaluation, we collect a new dataset, WHOLE, using a UAV platform across Weak-geometry, High-dynamic, Omnidirectional, Low-light, and Easy scenarios. Extensive experiments on public benchmarks and WHOLE demonstrate competitive rendering quality and improved geometric accuracy relative to perspective systems. Compared with existing panoramic baselines, GOLD-SLAM improves mean PSNR by 5.56-9.10 dB and reduces mean Depth-L1 by 71.9-83.9%. To assess deployment feasibility on resource-constrained platforms such as UAVs, we evaluate an embedded configuration of GOLD-SLAM on a Jetson Orin NX (16 GB), demonstrating online operation at the cost of reconstruction quality.</p></details> |  |

| **[D4GS: Efficient Gaussian Splatting SLAM with Depth Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36853v2)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Achieving high-quality reconstruction and responsive camera tracking remains challenging for monocular RGB Gaussian splatting SLAM under a limited optimization budget. | **[CoSphere-SLAM: Multi-Agent Panoramic Gaussian Splatting SLAM with Spherical Loop Closure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22235v2)** | 2026-11-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dense collaborative mapping is essential for search and rescue, building inspection, and warehouse automation, where panoramic cameras capture the entire surroundings in a single frame. Finally, omnidirectional Gaussian fusion transforms each Gaussian with its keyframe similarity and renders Gaussians at their maximum ray response to jointly refine poses and maps. Experiments on indoor and cross-session multi-agent panoramic benchmarks demonstrate that CoSphere-SLAM achieves state-of-the-art trajectory accuracy and rendering quality among single-agent and multi-agent methods. | **[Improved Accuracy Scaling with Problem Complexity in LLM Arithmetic Reasoning Using Executable Symbolic Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27203v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>High accuracy on mathematical benchmarks does not establish whether reasoning remains reliable as problem complexity increases. We examine how executable symbolic representations change accuracy scaling in large language models compared with natural-language reasoning. To address this limitation, we introduce the GSM Arithmetic Benchmark (GSM-AB), a controlled benchmark that independently varies operand digit length and operation count to probe numerical scale and compositional complexity. We find that executable representations can improve accuracy and slow its decline as complexity increases. Lean reduces average sensitivity to operand digit length at every tested model size. By contrast, slower degradation with increasing operation count emerges at the larger model sizes. A Python program-of-thought (Python-PoT) control shows a similar operation-axis pattern, suggesting that this finding is not specific to Lean. These findings show that reasoning representation shapes arithmetic accuracy scaling and support executable symbolic representations for more reliable reasoning on complex arithmetic tasks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Variants of Beam Search for DiskANN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26609v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Graph-based data structures such as DiskANN, HNSW, and NSG constitute the state of the art for large-scale approximate nearest-neighbor (ANN) search. Indyk and Xu introduced the notion of $α$-reachable graphs, which include graphs built by DiskANN's slow-preprocessing construction, and proved that greedy search achieves an approximation factor of $\tfrac{α+1}{α-1}$. Independently, Al-Jazzazi et al. proposed adaptive beam search (ABS) with a relaxation parameter $γ&gt; 0$, showing a $\tfrac{2}γ$ approximation on navigable graphs ($α\approx 1$), where greedy search admits no guarantees. In this work, we unify and generalize these results by proving that ABS with parameter $γ$ on $α$-reachable graphs achieves an approximation of $\tfrac{α+1}{α(γ+1)-1}$ for any metric. Moreover, on sorted $α$-reachable graphs (introduced by Gollapudi et al.) under the $\ell_2$ metric, we obtain the tighter bound $\max\bigl(\tfracα{α(γ+1)-1}, \tfrac{α^{2}+1}{α^{2}(γ+1)-1}\bigr)$. Beyond these theoretical results, we design a beam predictor using features inspired by fine-grained analysis of ABS, trained via a constrained objective that minimizes mean distance computations subject to a target recall. On three 100-million-point benchmarks, using graphs built by DiskANN's practical construction, beam predictor lowers mean query latency on SSD-resident indices at matched recall by up to $29.6\%$ relative to classical beam search (CBS). Relative to ABS, it is faster in $11$ of $15$ settings, by up to $6.5\%$, and at most $1.4\%$ slower in the other $4$; at $95\%$ recall, its advantage over ABS grows from $0.8$--$4.2\%$ for $100$ neighbors to $4.6$--$5.6\%$ for $500$ neighbors. In memory, it needs $1.3$--$23.7\%$ fewer distance computations than CBS and $0.0$--$3.9\%$ fewer than ABS.</p></details> | 22 pages, 1 figure, 9 tables |
| **[A Brief Overview of Non-Terrestrial Networks (Updated 2026)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25984v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This article extends the 2023 preprint \emph{A brief introduction to satellite communications for Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN)} (\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.04590v1}{arXiv:2305.04590v1}) with a revision of its foundations, tables and state of the art as of 18 November 2026. It revisits propagation, link budgets, spectrum reuse and phased arrays, including corrected equations and numerical examples. Updated tables distinguish operational, launched and planned constellations and compare published fixed-terminal rates with direct-to-device services. The discussion incorporates developments in 3GPP Releases 17--20, commercial LTE-based direct-to-cell services and research towards 6G non-terrestrial networks. Operator announcements are distinguished from independent measurements and future plans. Open challenges include spectrum coordination, Doppler and timing compensation, mobility, capacity, security and the sustainability of low Earth orbit.</p></details> |  |
| **[DAL: Dynamic Angular Loss for Imbalanced Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24597v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Class imbalance remains a major obstacle in medical image classification, where rare but clinically important classes are often overshadowed by majority categories. Although current strategies for reducing bias, including angle-based softmax (ABS) losses, offer strong discriminative features, their behavior in settings with a small number of severely imbalanced classes remains insufficiently understood. Previous approaches that estimate the intrinsic parameters of ABS rely on the assumption of many classes, which does not hold in medical settings. We derive data-driven upper and lower bounds for the scale parameter and an upper bound for the angular margin using negative-angle statistics, and use these to drive a batch-adaptive angular margin (BAAM), which we combine with dynamically-weighted cross entropy (DWCE) to define our dynamic angular loss (DAL). Across four highly imbalanced medical image classification benchmarks spanning dermatology, ophthalmology, and gastrointestinal endoscopy, our method consistently achieves superior balanced accuracy and the lowest coefficient of variation of per-class recall. Although this entails a small performance reduction on majority classes, the gains on minority categories are substantial, yielding a reliable and equitable overall classifier. Our source code is available at: https://github.com/msalman95/DAL.</p></details> | Accepted at the British Machine Vision Conference (BMVC) 2026. 18 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables. Code: https://github.com/msalman95/DA |

## relightable Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Patient-Zero: Scaling Synthetic Patient Agents to Real-World Distributions without Real Patient Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11078v3)** | 2026-11-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Synthetic data generation with Large Language Models (LLMs) has emerged as a promising solution in the medical domain to mitigate data scarcity and privacy constraints. However, existing approaches remain constrained by their derivative nature, relying on real-world records, which pose privacy risks and distribution biases. Furthermore, current patient agents face the Stability-Plasticity Dilemma, struggling to maintain clinical consistency during dynamic inquiries. To address these challenges, we introduce Patient-Zero, a novel framework for ab initio patient simulation that requires no real medical records. Our Medically-Aligned Hierarchical Synthesis framework generates comprehensive and diverse patient records from medical knowledge via stratified attribute permutation. To support rigorous clinical interaction, we design a Dual-Track Cognitive Memory System to enable agents dynamically update memory while preserving logical consistency and persona adherence. Extensive evaluations show that Patient-Zero outperforms baselines in both data quality and interaction fidelity. In physician evaluations, our records obtain the highest mean diversity, consistency, and completeness ratings. Downstream medical reasoning model trained on our synthetic dataset shows substantial performance gains (MedQA +24.0%; MMLU +14.5%), demonstrating the practical utility of our framework.</p></details> | 27 pages, 5 figures. Published in EMNLP 2026 |
| **[Information-Theoretic Generalization for Adaptive Learning Under Privacy and Fairness Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30867v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Adaptive learners may update, select, and stop models after repeatedly examining the same data. Privacy and fairness constraints regulate this adaptation only selectively: they limit information about a private or sensitive coordinate, whereas task generalization depends on information about the coordinate on which loss is evaluated. We develop a unified information-theoretic framework for this mismatch. For a finite-alphabet pair $(A,B)$, we define directly the residual-information certificate $ h_ε(P_{AB})=\min\{H(B),H(B&#124; A)+ε\}. $ A chain-rule transfer inequality converts a bound on $I(W;A)$ into an upper bound on $I(W;B)$, which can be inserted into a mutual-information generalization inequality. We first establish a transcript-level composition theorem for sequential learners. Conditional per-update information budgets add along the transcript, and the resulting certificate remains valid for history-dependent checkpoint selection, bounded stopping, and independent heterogeneous data batches. Under selective privacy, the learner observes $(X^n,Y^n)$, protects $X^n$, and incurs loss on $Y^n$. Under fairness, the learner observes $(S^n,X^n,T^n)$, limits dependence on the sensitive block $S^n$, compresses $X^n$, and incurs loss on $T^n$. The bounds expose the residual entropies $H(Y&#124; X)$, $H(T&#124; S)$, and $H(T&#124; X)$ and the cumulative information cost of adaptation. We retain block and individual-sample bounds, task-compression refinements, per-output total-variation privacy, empirical-to-population demographic-parity transfer, and finite-alphabet information-parity transfer. FRL/EFRL/SFRL/ESFRL constructions study the tightness of the task-information upper bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[Computing $ab+cd$ and other expressions with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30415v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We introduce two ``high-level'' algorithms that make it possible to evaluate the sum $(x\_h+x\_\ell) + (y\_h+y\_\ell)$ of two double-word numbers with correct rounding in binary floating-point arithmetic. These algorithms allow one to evaluate expressions of the form $ab+cd$, $a+b+cd$, $a+b+c+d$, and $abc$ with correct rounding, where $a$, $b$, $c$, and $d$ are floating-point numbers.</p></details> |  |
| **[NNS-GS: Non-Local Neural Scattering for Relightable Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30351v1)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Creating relightable 3D assets of translucent objects is challenging because changing the illumination at one surface location can affect the appearance elsewhere through subsurface scattering. Existing Gaussian-based scattering models typically predict scattering from local features, making this dependence difficult to capture. We present Non-Local Neural Scattering for Gaussian Splatting (NNS-GS), which learns non-local scattering together with geometry-guided local reflection. The scattering module learns how illumination from light-visible surface regions in light space contributes to the shading point in view space, conditioned on regional diffuse responses and spatial relationships. The reflection module supplies these diffuse responses and uses additional geometric guidance to better capture specular highlights and sharp shadow boundaries. To reduce component mixing and support controllable scattering appearance, anchor-based progressive training establishes the diffuse-scattering base before introducing specular reflection and supervises the components separately. Under calibrated point lighting, NNS-GS improves mean PSNR over existing methods by 2.47 dB and 0.81 dB on the synthetic and real translucent scenes, respectively. Additional synthetic comparisons further show that NNS-GS better matches the reference when adjusting scattering attenuation.</p></details> |  |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v3)** | 2026-11-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Sharp Volume Contraction of a Centrally Cut John Ellipsoid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29323v1)** | 2026-11-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$ in positive dimension, let $w(K)$ be the volume of its maximum-volume inscribed ellipsoid. For a closed halfspace $H$ whose boundary passes through the center of that ellipsoid, we prove \[ w(K\cap H)\le R_nw(K),\qquad R_1=\frac12,\qquad R_n=\frac12\left(\frac n{n-1}\right)^{(n-1)/2}\quad(n\ge2), \] with equality attained in every fixed dimension. This refines the optimal dimension-independent bound from the author's earlier work on [Khachiyan's ellipsoid conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28447). We characterize the full equality family, including nonsimplex extremizers, and establish an exact volume-deficit identity that yields dimension-independent stability of the new ellipsoid and its weighted contact geometry in John coordinates. The proof reduces the problem to finite John contacts and constructs an explicit conic certificate by a transformation of contact directions and a common polar rotation. Its determinant is controlled by a nonnegative contact defect. The sharp contraction theorem, equality classification, stability estimates, and supporting results have been formally verified in Lean 4 using mathlib.</p></details> | Lean 4 formalization with mathlib, see [github repository](https://github.com/DrZhouKarl/SharpContractionCutJohnEllipsoid) |
| **[ADEPT: Adaptive Monocular Depth Estimation via Model-In-The-Loop Photometric Tuning with Capture Risk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27423v1)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Exposure and gain determine the visual evidence available to monocular depth estimation (MDE), particularly when robot motion creates a tradeoff between light collection and motion blur. We present ADEPT, a framework that adapts these settings using learned feedback from a frozen depth model. During training, ground-truth depth identifies the lowest-error reference within a group of physically evaluated camera settings. A compact predictor learns reference-relative disagreement (RRD) between each candidate's depth prediction and the reference prediction using regression and ranking losses. We evaluate offline camera selection with relative and metric Depth Anything v2 and demonstrate online control with the affine-invariant variant on a mobile robot. Across nine equally weighted scene-illumination conditions, ADEPT reduces mean scale-and-shift-aligned $\mathrm{Abs}_{\mathrm{rel}}$ by 14.6% relative to built-in auto-exposure and by 10.0% relative to the best-performing evaluated prior controller.</p></details> |  |

These findings show that reasoning representation shapes arithmetic accuracy scaling and support executable symbolic representations for more reliable reasoning on complex arithmetic tasks.</p></details> |  |
